# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library agrees with the Rust reference bit for bit only if every group operation and pairing check it performs is proven against vectors the reference produced, read by Foundry tests from committed vector files, one encoded record per line; the established form for precompile vectors, in EIP-2537's reference vector files, execution-spec-tests, and go-ethereum's precompile fixtures, is a record holding the precompile's input bytes and the expected output, with the pairing check's vectors holding passing products built by bilinearity and failing products beside them, and multi-scalar multiplication taking any positive number of point-and-scalar pairs; each record here also carries the scalars it was drawn from, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state (CR-10 on-chain vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` and `<line>` entries for `contracts/evm/generated`; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope; EIP-196, EIP-197, and EIP-2537's input and output encodings)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a vector count and a multi-scalar term count are each a positive integer; zero is refused
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every scalar a record uses is drawn through the randomness family at the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares and sampled through that bound; a record's draws are each drawn scalar's encoding through the pairing's `encode_scalar`, concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a first-group addition record draws `a` and `b`; its input is the encodings of `a·g1` and `b·g1`, and its output is the encoding of their sum through `add_g1`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a first-group multiplication record draws `a` and the multiplier `s`; its input is the encoding of `a·g1` followed by the encoding of `s`, and its output is the encoding of `(a·g1)·s` through `mul_g1`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a pairing-check record draws `a` and `b`, and on a record at an odd index also `c`; its input is the encodings of the pairs `(a·g1, b·g2)` and `(t·g1, g2)`, each pair's first-group encoding followed by its second-group encoding, where `t` is `−(a·b)` at an even index and `c` at an odd index; its output is the precompile's 32-byte word, every byte zero except the last, which is `0x01` where `pairing_product_is_one` returns true and `0x00` otherwise
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a second-group addition record is the first-group addition record over `g2`, `add_g2`, and second-group encodings
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a multi-scalar multiplication record holds the configured number of terms; for each term it draws the base's scalar `a_i` and the multiplier `s_i`, its draws being `a_1`, `s_1`, `a_2`, `s_2`, and so on; its input is, per term in order, the encoding of `a_i·g` followed by the encoding of `s_i`; its output is the encoding of the sum through `msm_g1` or `msm_g2`, `g` being that group's generator
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every first-group kind and the pairing check hold exactly their configured counts on every pairing; the second-group kinds hold exactly their configured counts where the pairing declares `BothGroups`, and are absent where it declares `FirstGroupOnly`; second-group settings handed for a first-group-only pairing, and none handed for a both-groups pairing, are each refused before any draw
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw or a failed sampling is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count or a field of the wrong kind with the index, and states its member names, `draws`, `input`, and `output`, with each member's canonical field kind, as the record members `render` takes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, kind count, file, library, struct, or function name; every count and term count arrives in its params, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm/rejection_vectors` **The decode-rejection vector records, an enumerated kind: one record per candidate encoding the pairing family defines, the outside-the-subgroup point of each source group where that group is not the whole curve and the scalar field's order as the non-canonical scalar, each with what it decodes into and its bytes; owns the record type, its decode-target vocabulary, and its canonical description with its member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library performs subgroup checks where the precompile does not and must refuse every encoding the pairing family defines as invalid, so its Foundry tests read those encodings from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line, and expect each to be refused; the established form for precompile failure vectors, in EIP-2537's `fail-*` reference files, is a file of inputs every one of which must fail, so the kind itself states the expected outcome and no record carries one; the candidates are the pairing family's own reference encodings, whose refusal by every decoder the pairing family's own tests prove, so every library of a curve yields the same file (CR-10 subgroup rejection and encoding edge cases on chain; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/rejection_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for decode rejection; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope; `IPairingReference`'s outside-the-subgroup rule in `adapters/pairing/src/factory/interface.rs`)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the records are, in order, the first group's outside-the-subgroup encoding where `g1_outside_subgroup_encoding` returns one, the second group's outside-the-subgroup encoding from `g2_outside_subgroup_encoding`, and the scalar field's order's bytes from `scalar_field_order`; nothing else
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record names its decode target, `G1`, `G2`, or `Scalar`, and holds the candidate's bytes unchanged as its input
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed outside-the-subgroup search is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a decode target admits exactly its names and refuses any other text with the text
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count, a field of the wrong kind with the index, and an unknown target name with the refusal, and states its member names, `target` and `input`, with each member's canonical field kind, as the record members `render` takes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function draws no randomness, reads no clock, touches no filesystem, and decodes nothing; it names no curve or concrete; every candidate is the selected pairing's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the EVM concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, and `group_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the record type, the decode-target type, and the record's canonical description with its member names
    * `[✅]`   Does not decode a candidate; each decoder's refusal of the family's candidates is proven in the pairing family's own tests
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/evm` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector file, and builds the kind's record declaration from the description's members
    * `[✅]`   Does not compute a candidate; the pairing family's reference trait supplies every one
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing; `harness-crypto/run` obtains it through the pairing factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapter
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm/mapping_vectors` **The hash-to-scalar vector records, a drawn kind, each drawn message mapped under every domain tag the constants library carries, and the identity-mapping vector records, a kind over configured inputs, each identity's bytes mapped through the credential KEM under the admitted identity scope and read from the identity element's components; owns both record types and their canonical descriptions with their member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity verifier recomputes the identity mapping, the proofs of possession's challenges, and the delivery proof's challenge and weight as keccak256 under a domain tag reduced modulo the group order, so its Foundry tests read the reference's mappings from committed vector files; the established form for hash-to-field test vectors, in RFC 9380's appendix vectors, is a domain separation tag, a message, and the resulting field element, every message taken under the suite's tag, so mapping one message under every tag the contracts hash under proves each tag is bound; the identity mapping is proven through the KEM itself, the scalar read from the identity element it derives, so the vector is what the issuer and the contract both compute (CR-08 and CR-11 identity-mapping and hash-to-scalar vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/mapping_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entries for hash-to-scalar and identity mapping; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the domain tags are, in order, the identity mapping's from the KEM's declaration, the first- and second-group proofs of possession's from the key-agreement declaration, and the delivery proof's challenge and weight from the delivery-proof declaration, each admitted as a `DomainTag`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each of the configured count of messages, the function draws a message of the configured length through the randomness family and emits one hash-to-scalar record per tag in tag order, each holding the tag's bytes, the message, and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar `hash_to_scalar` returns for that tag and message
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each identity-bytes value the payload carries, in order, the function emits one identity-mapping record holding the bytes and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar of the identity element `derive_identity` returns under the admitted scope, read through `identity_element_components`; under the entitlement scope the bytes are the canonical identity and the parameter set is set up for the entitlement scope; under the asset scope the bytes are admitted as an `AssetIdentityHash`, a value not 32 bytes long refused with its index and length, and the parameter set is set up for that asset
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each parameter set is set up from three uniform draws of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; its draws do not reach the record, since the identity element's scalar is a function of the tag and the bytes alone
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused tag, a failed draw, a refused asset identity, and a failed hash, setup, or derivation are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count or a field of the wrong kind with the index, and states its member names with each member's canonical field kind as the record members `render` takes: `tag`, `message`, and `scalar` for the hash-to-scalar record, and `identity` and `scalar` for the identity-mapping record
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, tag, count, length, scope, or identity; every tag is read from a declaration, every count and length arrives in its params, the scope is the one the KEM factory admitted, and every identity arrives in its payload

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the EVM concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, `group_vectors`, and `rejection_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the hash-to-scalar and identity-mapping record types and their canonical descriptions with their member names; consumes `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` owns
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/evm` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector files, and builds each kind's record declaration from the description's members
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose a count, a message length, a scope, or an identity; the configuration carries the count, the length, and the identities, the KEM factory admits the scope, and `harness-crypto/generate/evm` passes them here
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, KEM, or randomness source; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each through its factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapters and the declarations
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm/possession_vectors` **The possession vector records, a drawn kind: envelope key pairs generated through the key-agreement family from secret draws the function makes, each record carrying the drawn secrets, the public keys, and the proofs of possession's components, the proofs' nonces drawn inside the adapter and not recorded; owns the record type and its canonical description with its member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: envelope-key registration verifies a Schnorr proof of possession per key on chain, so the contract's verification must agree with the Rust reference on keys and proofs the reference generated; its Foundry tests read them from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line, each record carrying the inputs the generator chose, the secrets, and the outputs a verifier checks, the public keys and each proof's commitment and response, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state (LC-08 at the algebra level, on chain; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/possession_vectors` row; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for possession; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each of the configured count of records, the function draws two uniforms through the randomness family at the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares and generates a key pair with its proofs of possession through the key agreement's `generate_keys`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds the key pair's secrets `x` and `y`, read through `key_pair_components`, each as its 32-byte big-endian encoding; the public keys `pk1` and `pk2`, read through `key_pair_public_keys`, in the pairing's first- and second-group precompile encodings; and the proofs' components `r1`, `z1`, `r2`, and `z2`, read through `possession_components`, the commitments in the precompile encodings and the responses as 32-byte big-endian encodings
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw and a failed key generation are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a scalar the pairing encodes to other than 32 bytes is refused with the encoding's length
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count or a field of the wrong kind with the index, and states its member names, `x`, `y`, `pk1`, `pk2`, `r1`, `z1`, `r2`, and `z2`, with each member's canonical field kind, as the record members `render` takes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, tag, or count; the count arrives in its params, the proofs' challenges are the key agreement's own under the tags its declaration carries, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the EVM concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, `group_vectors`, `rejection_vectors`, and `mapping_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns the possession record type and its canonical description with its member names; consumes `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` owns
    * `[✅]`   Does not compute a proof of possession or its challenge; the key agreement generates and proves the keys
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/evm` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector file, and builds the kind's record declaration from the description's members
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, key agreement, or randomness source; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each through its factory and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapters
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm/challenge_vectors` **The challenge vector records, a drawn kind, generic over the selected pairing and the chain's forms: for each of the configured count, a standalone mint transcript and a standalone transfer transcript whose forms are rebuilt from the payload's canonical context values and whose every point is a generator times a scalar the function draws, each record carrying its purpose, its draws, the transcript's encoding through its description and the encoding family, and its challenge, the hash-to-scalar mapping of that encoding under the challenge tag the delivery-proof declaration carries; owns the record type and its canonical description with its member names, and the canonical delivery context the configuration supplies**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity verifier recomputes the delivery proof's challenge as the hash-to-scalar mapping of the transcript's canonical encoding under the challenge tag, so its Foundry tests read the reference's transcripts, their encodings, and their challenges from a committed vector file, one encoded record per line; the proof family's challenge function is private to its crate and a proof in the second-group form carries no first messages, so these transcripts stand alone, built by this function over the same public transcript descriptions the proof family encodes through, their context values taken from the configuration and their points from scalars the function draws and records, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state; the proofs' own challenges travel in the proof vectors (CR-09 and CR-11 challenge vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/challenge_vectors` row and its conflict flag on standalone challenge transcripts; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for challenge; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope and its exit, the challenge vectors mirrored to Solidity and reproduced by the deployed verifier)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a delivery-statement version other than the one the transcript descriptions serve is refused with the version before any other step
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the challenge tag is read from the delivery-proof declaration and admitted as a `DomainTag`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each canonical context's chain identifier, entitlement contract, source and target entitlements, old and new intervals, seller, and buyer are rebuilt as the chain's forms from their canonical field values before any draw; a form's refusal is returned unchanged with the relation whose context carried it and, for every field but the chain identifier, the field
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for each index from zero below the configured count, the function emits a mint record then a transfer record; a mint transcript holds the mint context, the mint purpose, and eleven points, its buyer keys, envelope, and first messages; a transfer transcript holds the transfer context, the transfer purpose, and eighteen points, its seller keys, buyer keys, old and new envelopes, and first messages
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every point is drawn as a uniform of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares, sampled into a scalar through that bound, and multiplied onto its group's generator; a record's draws are each drawn scalar's 32-byte big-endian encoding, concatenated in the order the transcript's description lists its group fields
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds its purpose's code, its draws, the bytes the encoding family encodes its transcript to under the transcript's description, and the 32-byte big-endian encoding of the scalar `hash_to_scalar` returns for those bytes under the challenge tag
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused tag, a failed draw, a failed sampling, a failed encoding, and a failed hash are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count or a field of the wrong kind with the index, and states its member names, `purpose`, `draws`, `encoding`, and `challenge`, with each member's canonical field kind, as the record members `render` takes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, tag, count, chain, or context value; the count and version arrive in its params, the tag in the declaration, the context values and purposes in its payload, and every encoding is the selected pairing's and the encoding family's

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm/proof_vectors` **The mint-proof and transfer-proof vector records, each a drawn kind, generic over the selected pairing, the credential KEM, the key agreement, and the delivery proof with its chain forms: per mint record a parameter set set up under the admitted identity scope, the identity element of the configured identity, a credential issued and wrapped to a seller's admitted keys, and the mint prover's proof; per transfer record the same mint, the seller's fresh decryption rerandomized and wrapped to a buyer's admitted keys, and the transfer prover's proof; each record its draws, its statement as its context values and its elements' precompile encodings, and its proof's components, the first-group-only form carrying its second-group first messages; owns both record types, their shared context type, and their canonical descriptions with their member names**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity delivery verifier must accept exactly what the Rust reference verifier accepts, bit for bit, so its Foundry tests read mint and transfer statements with proofs the reference produced from committed vector files, one encoded record per line; a statement reaches the contract as its context values and its group elements, and a proof as its challenge and responses, with the three second-group first messages added where the verifier has only first-group arithmetic, so each record carries those and the uniforms the function drew, as Foundry's failure persistence records a run's inputs rather than its generator state; the parameter-set digest and the identity bytes are configured values, since no harness ticket computes a digest and the verifier takes the identity element as an input (CR-09 parity inputs; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm/proof_vectors` row and its conflict flag on the harness's configured parameter-set digest and identity bytes; the technical requirements' `<records>` entry for mint and transfer proofs; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope and its exit, the Rust verifier and the deployed contract agreeing on every vector)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each canonical context's chain identifier, entitlement contract, source and target entitlements, old and new intervals, seller, and buyer are rebuilt as the delivery proof's forms from their canonical field values for every record of that context's relation, before that record's draws; a form's refusal is returned unchanged with the relation and, for every field but the chain identifier, the field
    * `[✅]`   Functional: under the asset scope the configured identity is admitted as an `AssetIdentityHash` before any record, a value not 32 bytes long refused with its length; under the entitlement scope it is the canonical identity
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each mint record sets up a parameter set from three uniforms, derives the identity element of the configured identity, issues a credential from a fourth uniform, generates a seller key pair from two more and admits its public keys with their proof of possession, wraps the credential to them, and proves the mint statement over the mint context, the mint purpose, the parameter set's `hpub`, the identity element, the seller's public keys as the recipient keys, and the envelope
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each transfer record performs the same setup, derivation, issuance, seller key generation, and wrap, then generates a buyer key pair from two uniforms and admits its public keys, unwraps the seller's envelope with the seller's key pair, rerandomizes the credential from a ninth uniform, wraps it to the buyer's public keys, and proves the transfer statement over the transfer context, the transfer purpose, the identity element, both parties' public keys, and the old and new envelopes from the seller's secrets, the offset, and the new envelope's coins
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; a record's draws are its uniforms concatenated in draw order; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not recorded
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record holds its context values as the canonical fields its transcript carries them in, its group elements in the pairing's precompile encodings, and its proof's challenge and responses as 32-byte big-endian encodings, with its second-group first messages' encodings concatenated in the first-group-only form and no bytes in the both-groups form
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused form, a refused asset identity, and a failed draw, setup, derivation, issuance, key generation, key admission, wrap, rerandomization, or proof are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record type's canonical description maps the record to its fields in member order, the context's form-typed fields at the kinds the delivery proof's forms declare, rebuilds the record from its fields, refusing a wrong field count or a field of the wrong kind with the index, and states its member names with each member's canonical field kind as the record members `render` takes
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, or context value; the counts and scope arrive in its params, the contexts, purposes, and identity in its payload, and every encoding is the selected pairing's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the EVM concrete of the harness's generate family owns, beside `render`, `constants`, `group_vectors`, `rejection_vectors`, `mapping_vectors`, `possession_vectors`, and `challenge_vectors` beneath the concrete's module
    * `[✅]`   Owns `ProofVectorContext`, `MintProofVector`, `TransferProofVector`, and their canonical descriptions with their member names; consumes `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` owns, and `CanonicalDeliveryContext` and `DeliveryContextField`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/challenge_vectors` owns
    * `[✅]`   Proves through the delivery-proof adapter it is handed; does not compute a challenge, a response, or a first message, and does not verify
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a record, write a file, or render a library; `harness-crypto/generate/evm` encodes each record through the encoding factory under its description, writes the vector files, and builds each kind's record declaration from the description's members
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose a count, a scope, a purpose, an identity, or a context value; the configuration carries them, the KEM factory admits the scope, and `harness-crypto/generate/evm` passes them here
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, KEM, key agreement, delivery proof, randomness source, or forms; `harness-crypto/run` and its consumers obtain each through its factory or `chain/create_chain_forms` and the generate concrete hands this function the borrowed adapters
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/generate/evm` **The EVM concrete of the harness's generate family, one adapter over the borrowed pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, randomness source, credential KEM, key agreement, and delivery proof with its chain forms: calls each function it owns with each kind's admitted size, encodes every vector record into one `0x`-prefixed hex line, renders the constants library and the vector-record library through `render`, and writes every vector file and both libraries under the configured generated directory in the directory configured for the declared curve; the family's only module that touches the filesystem; authors, as its producers, the generate family's interface, the vector-kind identifier, the configured per-kind quantities type, the admitted per-kind size type, the declaration with each kind's sizing and second-group gate, and the family's mock in the family's `factory` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Solidity pairing library and delivery verifier read their constants and test vectors from a committed generated directory written from the Rust reference, never by hand; per curve it holds a constants library the suite's contracts import, one vector file per declared kind holding one encoded record per line, and a vector-record library declaring per kind the record struct, the function decoding one line into it, and the constant holding the path of the kind's file relative to the Foundry project root; every name, count, path, compiler version, and license is the configuration's, and the curve directory is read from the curve the selected pairing declares, never chosen (CR-09 parity inputs; CR-10 and CR-11 on-chain vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/generate/evm` row and its conflict flags on committed vector files, declared and configured vector kinds, configured compiler version and license, the curve directory, and the generated directory; the technical requirements' file tree for `contracts/evm/generated`, its `<vector files>`, `<line>`, and `<records>` entries; the Delivery proof and Solidity verifier milestone's scope and its exit, each declared kind's vector file holding its configured count, its enumerated set, or one record per configured input, every line decoding through the vector-record library's function for that kind)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's declaration names the kinds a concrete emits, each with its sizing, drawn, drawn with a term count, drawn with a message length, enumerated, or over configured inputs, and whether it needs second-group arithmetic at the verifier; the EVM concrete declares first-group addition, first-group multiplication, the pairing check, second-group addition, first- and second-group multi-scalar multiplication, decode rejection, hash-to-scalar, identity mapping, possession, challenge, mint proof, and transfer proof, the second-group addition and both multi-scalar multiplications gated on second-group arithmetic

    * `[✅]`   Functional: reading the per-kind entries in order, a kind the declaration does not name is refused with the kind, a kind already named by an earlier entry is refused with the kind, and a kind whose configured quantities do not match its declared sizing is refused with the kind and the quantity, a quantity the sizing requires and the entry lacks and a quantity the entry holds and the sizing does not take, checked count, then terms, then message length; each admitted entry's size is built from its declared sizing and its quantities; then a declared kind no entry names is refused with the kind, in declared order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: admitted settings construct the concrete over the deps' adapters, the params' scope and statement version, the selection's settings, and the admitted sizes in declared order, and the family's mock concrete over its failure mode and the params' scope, so every concrete the factory constructs decides the family's asset identity admission under the scope the KEM factory admitted, and the consumer's output for that concrete is returned; nothing is constructed and the consumer is not called on a refusal
